\end {aligned}
$

                                            &= \pounds 8.85
\end {aligned}

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the cost of $\text{two}$ sandwiches and $\text{five}$ cups of coffee.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Cost of} \ 2 \ \text {sandwiches and} \ 5 \ \text {cups of coffee}   &= 2 \times \pounds 1.80 + 5 \times 80 \text {p} \\\\
                                                                            &= \pounds 3.60 + 5 \times \pounds 0.80 \\\\
                                                                            &= 3.60 + 0.80 \\\\
                                                                            &= \pounds 4.40
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Cost of} \ 2 \ \text {sandwiches and} \ 5 \ \text {cups of coffee}   &= 2 \times \pounds 1.80 + 5 \times 80 \text {p} \\\\
                                                                            &= \pounds 3.60 + 5 \times \pounds 0.80 \\\\
                                                                            &= 3.60 + 0.80 \\\\
                                                                            &= \pounds 4.40
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 4.40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many cups of tea can be bought with $\pounds5$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Cups of tea}     &= \dfrac {\pounds 5} {85 \text {p}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {500 \text {p}} {85 \text {p}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {500} {85} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 100} {5 \times 17} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 100} {\cancel {5} \times 17} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {100} {17} \\\\
                        &= 5 \ \text {remainder} \ 15 \\\\
                        &\approx 5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Cups of tea}     &= \dfrac {\pounds 5} {85 \text {p}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {500 \text {p}} {85 \text {p}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {500} {85} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 100} {5 \times 17} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 100} {\cancel {5} \times 17} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {100} {17} \\\\
                        &= 5 \ \text {remainder} \ 15 \\\\
                        &\approx 5
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$20\:\:\:  17\:\:\:  14\:\:\: .……..\:\:\: ………\:\:\: ………$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by subtracting $3$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin {matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-3)}}  \\
20,  &&    17,  &&  14,  &&   \ldots,  &&   \ldots, && \ldots
\end {matrix}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {First missing number}       &= 14 - 3 \\\\
                                   &= 11 \\\\
\text {Second missing number}      &= 11 - 3 \\\\
                                   &= 8 \\\\
\text {Third missing number}       &= 8 - 3 \\\\
                                   &= 5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note

The next term is calculated by subtracting $3$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-AB151B1F-7B35-4FF4-AC18-4876BE694A05'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

An equilateral triangle has three sides the same length. What is the size of one of the angles? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{All angles of an equilateral triangle are equal}$.


Let the angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + a              &= 180 \degree \\\\
3a                     &= 180 \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {180} {3} \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {3 \times 60} {3} \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 60} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
a                      &= 60 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Angle $= 60 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle

rtq_abbr_md_angles_of_an_equilateral_triangle

Let the angle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + a              &= 180 \degree \\\\
3a                     &= 180 \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {180} {3} \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {3 \times 60} {3} \\\\
a                      &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 60} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
a                      &= 60 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Angle $= 60 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is $\dfrac{1}{4}$ of ${68}$ ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {4} \ \text{of} \ 68 \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {4} \times 68 \\\\
&= \dfrac {68} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times 4} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
&= 17
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {4} \ \text{of} \ 68 \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {4} \times 68 \\\\
&= \dfrac {68} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times 4} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
&= 17
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='workings'>

\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3} {5} \ \text{of} \ 20 \\\\

&= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 5}{5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times4\times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
&=3\times4\\\\
&= 12
\end{aligned}
$

&= \dfrac {3 \times4\times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\

the prime factorization of 20

$ = 5$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In a survey $30\%$ of people were left handed only, $10\%$ were both left and right handed. What percentage were right handed only ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {\% Right handed only}    &= 100 - 30 - 10 \\\\
                                &= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {\% Right handed only}    &= 100 - 30 - 10 \\\\
                                &= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $50\%$ of $80$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 50 \% \text { of } 80 \\\\
&= \dfrac {50}{100} \times 80 \\\\
&= \dfrac {50 \times 80 }{100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \cancel {0} \times 8 \cancel{0} } {1 \cancel {0} \cancel {0}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 8 \\\\
&= 40 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 50 \% \text { of } 80 \\\\
&= \dfrac {50}{100} \times 80 \\\\
&= \dfrac {50 \times 80 }{100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \cancel {0} \times 8 \cancel{0} } {1 \cancel {0} \cancel {0}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 8 \\\\
&= 40 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>
</li>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many pieces of ribbon of $30\ \text{cm}$ length can be cut from a piece of length $7\ \text{m}$ ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total ribbons} &= \dfrac{7 \times 100 \ \text{cm}}{30 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
&= \dfrac{70 \times 2 \times 5}{3 \times 2 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{70 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5}{3 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{70}{3} \\\\
&\approx 23
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$23$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total ribbons} &= \dfrac{7 \times 100 \ \text{cm}}{30 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
&= \dfrac{70 \times 2 \times 5}{3 \times 2 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{70 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5}{3 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{70}{3} \\\\
&\approx 23
\end{aligned}
$

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$3x + 7 = 46$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
3x + 7                                              &= 46 \\\\
3x                                                  &= 46 - 7 \\\\
3x                                                  &= 39 \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {39} {3} \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 13} {3} \\\\
x                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 13} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
x                                                   &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='answer'>

$13$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-00CCC3EA-C140-4A54-BC78-9715614128A9'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Mrs Suzie needs to cover a floor measuring $3\ \text{m}$ by $2\ \text{m}$. There are three possible options:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
     ![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
    ![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Which option is the cheapest? Show all your workings & explain clearly. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
.
The floor measurement is given in $\text{m}$ while the tile measurement is given in $\text{cm}$.

Let's convert the floor measurements into $\text{cm}$.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of Acron tiles}       &= \dfrac {\text {Area of floor}} {\text {Area of a tile}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \ \text {m} \times 2 \ \text {m}} {50 \ \text {cm} \times 50 \ \text {cm}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 100 \ \text {cm} \times 2 \times 100 \ \text {cm}} {50 \ \text {cm} \times 50 \ \text {cm}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 100 \times 2 \times 100} {50 \times 50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 2 \times 2 \times 50 \times 2} {50 \times 50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 2 \times 2 \times \cancel {50} \times 2} {\cancel {50} \times \cancel {50}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 2 \times 2 \times 2 \\\\
                                    &= 24 \\\\
\text {Total cost of Acron tiles}   &= 4 \times 24 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds \ 96
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of Beeching tiles}        &= \dfrac {\text {Area of floor}} {\text {Area of a tile}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \ \text {m} \times 2 \ \text {m}} {60 \ \text {cm} \times 40 \ \text {cm}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 100 \ \text {cm} \times 2 \times 100 \ \text {cm}} {60 \ \text {cm} \times 40 \ \text {cm}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 10 \times 10 \times 2 \times 4 \times 25} {3 \times 2 \times 10 \times 4 \times 10} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {4} \times 25} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                                        &= 25
\text {Total cost of Beeching tiles}    &= 3 \times 25 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds \ 75
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total cost of carpet}            &= \text {Cost per square metre} \times \text {Area of floor} + \text {Fitting cost} \\\\
                                        &= 14 \times (3 \times 2) + 30 \\\\ 
                                        &= 14 \times 6 + 30 \\\\ 
                                        &= 84 + 30 \\\\ 
                                        &= \pounds \ 114
\end{aligned}
$

Beeching tiles are the cheapest.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Beeching tiles

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note.
The floor measurement is given in $\text{m}$ while the tile measurement is given in $\text{cm}$.

Let's convert the floor measurements into $\text{cm}$.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of Acron tiles}       &= \dfrac {\text {Area of floor}} {\text {Area of a tile}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \ \text {m} \times 2 \ \text {m}} {50 \ \text {cm} \times 50 \ \text {cm}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 100 \ \text {cm} \times 2 \times 100 \ \text {cm}} {50 \ \text {cm} \times 50 \ \text {cm}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 100 \times 2 \times 100} {50 \times 50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 2 \times 2 \times 50 \times 2} {50 \times 50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 2 \times 2 \times \cancel {50} \times 2} {\cancel {50} \times \cancel {50}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 2 \times 2 \times 2 \\\\
                                    &= 24 \\\\
\text {Total cost of Acron tiles}   &= 4 \times 24 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds \ 96
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of Beeching tiles}        &= \dfrac {\text {Area of floor}} {\text {Area of a tile}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \ \text {m} \times 2 \ \text {m}} {60 \ \text {cm} \times 40 \ \text {cm}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 100 \ \text {cm} \times 2 \times 100 \ \text {cm}} {60 \ \text {cm} \times 40 \ \text {cm}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 10 \times 10 \times 2 \times 4 \times 25} {3 \times 2 \times 10 \times 4 \times 10} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {4} \times 25} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                                        &= 25
\text {Total cost of Beeching tiles}    &= 3 \times 25 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds \ 75
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total cost of carpet}            &= \text {Cost per square metre} \times \text {Area of floor} + \text {Fitting cost} \\\\
                                        &= 14 \times (3 \times 2) + 30 \\\\ 
                                        &= 14 \times 6 + 30 \\\\ 
                                        &= 84 + 30 \\\\ 
                                        &= \pounds \ 114
\end{aligned}
$

Beeching tiles are the cheapest.

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-5F079E8F-761B-4703-9613-DF5D0E436C2F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

In a dancing competition, the $\text{four}$ judges gave marks ranging from $–5 to 5$, (whole numbers). There were $4$ competitors.

Andrew got $\underline{top\ \ \text{marks}}$ from every judge. 

Brian’s score was $4$ less than Andrew. 

Carla and Donna both had the same final score of $7$.

Complete the table:

|               | Judge $1$     | Judge $2$     | Judge $3$     | Judge $4$     | Total         |
|--------       |:---------:    |:---------:    |:---------:    |:---------:    |:-----:        |
| Andrew        |               |               |               |               |               |
| Brian         |    $5$        |    $4$        |    $3$        |               |               |
| Carla         |    $4$        |    $2$        |               |    $0$        |               |
| Donna         |    $0$        |    $-3$       |    $5$        |               |               |  

</div>
